## Appendix A: lead decisions and drafting deviations

### A.0 Lead decisions (these override anything below that disagrees)

1. **The shared fixture shape is part A's.**
   - Shape: `{"rule_sets": {name: [rule snapshots]}, "cases": [{"name", "rules": <rule_sets key>, "query": {"peptide_id", "weight_mg", "is_bac", "endo_prep", "rules_off"}, "expect": <rule id or null>}]}`.
   - The winner is the minimum of `(width, sort_order, id)`.
   - "No weight" means `weight_mg is None`; 0 mg is a weight.
2. **Re-apply candidates: the server is the authority.**
   - A row is a candidate when its locked line differs from today's match by line id (including a line against none), or when a value differs on a field the row does not type (part B `_moves`).
   - Task 8's dialog lists exactly the server's candidates. Drop part C's own client-side filter (the block documented as "fields are all typed would gain nothing", around Task 8's `ReapplyRules`), and assert that the dialog shows what the preview returns.
   - A line-only change with identical numbers is listed, so the row can take the new line's provenance.
3. **The switch lock (B3).** Switching a row back to rules locks it at once when the sheet was printed or the row has a result (`locked_by: "switch"`). This extends spec section 6 and closes part C's gap. Switching to "no rule" drops the lock; when the row has a result, the dropped lock is kept in the override history entry.
4. **A promoted row's prep is final (B4, spec section 8).**
   - The item PATCH refuses a change to `prep_total_dilution`, `prep_steps`, `cartridge_type_id`, `prep_rules_off` or `prep_note` with 409 `the row is promoted; its prep is final`.
   - Readings and other fields keep today's behaviour.
5. **A rule's cartridge written at the first reading counts as typed from then on.**
   - Box 3 and the reader import already send the row's resolved cartridge with the reading. The reading was taken on that physical cartridge, so Re-apply must not move it.
   - That first-reading PATCH is not an override: part B tests `item.result_reading is not None` on the state before the change.
6. **Ruled steps are not boxed on the bench sheet.** This follows the dispatch (part C deviation 4).
   - Dennis's own sheet boxes them. Reverting is one line in `boxOf`, plus two test assertions.
   - Flag it in UAT for Dennis.
7. **Box 2 sends `[30, null]` for a step 1 ratio nobody typed** (part C deviation 5). The server reads it as 1:10, as it did `[30, 10]`. Storing the untyped ratio as typed would block every future rule on that row.
8. **`row_facts.is_bac` is the form rule:** `prep_form == "bac"` when `prep_form` is set, else the sample rule with slice 3's identity fallback. A bac row is never ruled, even one locked before its form was set (part C's note). The Python effective values skip the rule for bac in the same way.
9. **The constants.** `worksheets_endo.STEP1_STANDARD_RATIO` stays (part A's test pins `endo_prep_rules.STEP1_RATIO` to it); only `_step1_ratio` is removed (B6).
10. **Accepted part A deviations.**
    - A rule on a blend catalog peptide is not refused. A blend sample never matches anyway, because its `sample_peptide_id` is None.
    - A rule blocks deleting its peptide, as a spec row already does.
    - The first production boot logs one `migration_skipped` for the new `CREATE TABLE` before `create_all` builds it. Add this to the launch checklist so the operator expects it.

11. **Rollback materialisation (spec section 11).** Before any rollback to an image without slice 5, write each locked rule's values into the row's typed columns wherever they are empty, so the old image computes the same numbers. `prep_rule` and `prep_steps` are JSON columns.
    - The read-only check comes first:
      ```sql
      SELECT id, worksheet_id, sample_id, prep_rule->'rule' AS rule
      FROM worksheet_items
      WHERE prep_rule IS NOT NULL AND json_typeof(prep_rule->'rule') = 'object' AND NOT prep_rules_off;
      ```
    - The update runs in one transaction:
      ```sql
      BEGIN;
      UPDATE worksheet_items SET prep_total_dilution = (prep_rule->'rule'->>'total_dilution')::float
       WHERE prep_total_dilution IS NULL AND NOT prep_rules_off
         AND json_typeof(prep_rule->'rule') = 'object' AND prep_rule->'rule'->>'total_dilution' IS NOT NULL;
      UPDATE worksheet_items SET cartridge_type_id = (prep_rule->'rule'->>'cartridge_type_id')::int
       WHERE cartridge_type_id IS NULL AND NOT prep_rules_off
         AND json_typeof(prep_rule->'rule') = 'object' AND prep_rule->'rule'->>'cartridge_type_id' IS NOT NULL;
      UPDATE worksheet_items
         SET prep_steps = json_build_array(json_build_array(NULL, (prep_rule->'rule'->>'step1_ratio')::float), NULL, NULL)
       WHERE prep_steps IS NULL AND NOT prep_rules_off
         AND json_typeof(prep_rule->'rule') = 'object' AND prep_rule->'rule'->>'step1_ratio' IS NOT NULL;
      UPDATE worksheet_items
         SET prep_steps = jsonb_set(prep_steps::jsonb, '{0}',
               jsonb_build_array(prep_steps::jsonb->0->0, (prep_rule->'rule'->>'step1_ratio')::float))::json
       WHERE prep_steps IS NOT NULL AND (prep_steps::jsonb->0->>1) IS NULL AND NOT prep_rules_off
         AND json_typeof(prep_rule->'rule') = 'object' AND prep_rule->'rule'->>'step1_ratio' IS NOT NULL;
      COMMIT;
      ```
    - **Rehearsal.** Task 10 rehearses it on `endos3` inside `BEGIN; ... ROLLBACK;`. Before the rollback, confirm that a ruled row's typed columns now hold its rule's values.
    - **Launch checklist and PR.** The launch checklist's rollback order gains this as its first step, before slice 4 and slice 3. The PR body carries it.

### A.1 Part A (Tasks 1-2)

None of these changes a contract name, signature or shape. Each one is an addition or a reading of the spec that the other parts and the Handler should see.

1. **File Structure.** Task 2 also modifies `backend/endo_prep_rules.py`: it adds `check_rule` with the private helpers `_bounds`, `_band_text` and `_active_endo_methods`. The table lists only `main.py` for Task 2. The save checks live beside the matcher, the way `check_item_endo_fields` lives in `worksheets_endo.py`, rather than in main.py.
2. **New names beside the contract's.** These live in `main.py`: `EndoPrepRulePatch` (the PATCH body), `EndoPrepRuleSaveOut` (the `{rule, warnings}` shape) and `EndoPrepRuleReorderIn`.
3. **The matcher's final tie-break is `id`.** Spec section 4 stops at the lowest `sort_order`, which nothing makes unique. The key `(width, sort_order, id)` makes equal lines deterministic on both sides, and a fixture case pins it, so Task 6 must use the same key.
4. **"The active Endotoxin method revision" (spec section 3 warning) has no worksheet at save time.** Task 2 reads it as every active method that is the default for an endotoxin-keyword service.
   - With none, as on prod until the launch step sets method 26's default, there is no cartridge warning, and the ratio-only floor uses 250.
   - With several, each one that refuses the cartridge gets its own warning, and the floor uses the smallest standard.
   - A worksheet pinned to an older revision may have another standard. The server floor check in Task 3 (`check_item_endo_fields` on effective values) stays the real guard.
5. **Peptide FK with no ON DELETE**, as `analysis_service_specs.peptide_id`.
   - Once a rule names a peptide, `DELETE /peptides/{id}` and `DELETE /peptides/wipe-all` fail on Postgres with an IntegrityError, which is a 500, as they already do for a peptide that has a spec row.
   - SQLite tests do not show this. It is not fixed here.
6. **The boot statement on the first boot.** `_run_migrations` runs before `create_all`. On a database without `consumable_types` (prod, whose first boot of this release also ships v2), the `CREATE TABLE` logs one `migration_skipped`, and `create_all` builds the table from the model. Task 10 should expect that warning on a stack restored from a pre-v2 golden, and should check `\d endo_prep_rules` there.
7. **Three existing test files run the whole boot list on the host dev Postgres at import:** `test_sla_schema.py`, `test_priority_migration.py` and `test_priority_embed.py`. After Task 1 they would create `endo_prep_rules` on the host. They are kept out of every gate here and belong in Task 10 on the stack. Parts B and C should not run them either.
8. **A blend catalog entity is not refused as a rule's peptide.** Spec section 3 lists no such check. Such a line matches only a sample whose single anchor is that blend id. If the Handler wants it refused, it is one check in `check_rule` (`Peptide.is_blend`).

### A.2 Part B (Tasks 3-5)

The plan lead accepted B1, B2, B3, B5, B6, B7 and B8 as rulings, and ruled B4: build it. They stay listed here so the plan's appendix carries them.

**B1. Re-apply's candidate test (spec 7, "the row's snapshot differs from today's match").**
- *Problem:* Read literally, the test lists two things it should not:
  - a locked row with a typed override whenever its line's values change. That contradicts Review Focus 2 ("Re-apply lists only the ruled row") and D6 ("Re-apply never touches an override").
  - every locked row after a reorder, status or note edit, because `snapshot` carries `sort_order`, `status` and `note`.
- *Built (`_moves`):* A row is listed when its locked line id differs from today's match (including a line against none), or when a value differs on a field the row does not type. One helper serves both the GET and the POST's "unchanged" refusal.
- *Proposal:* Keep this. The spec 7 bullet could read "Re-apply would change the line or an untyped value".

**B2. The POST's "changed since the preview" (spec 7).**
- *Problem:* The body is `{"item_ids"}` only, so the server cannot see what the preview showed.
- *Built:* Apply refuses a row that would change nothing now (`REAPPLY_UNCHANGED`). If the line moved again between preview and apply, the row takes today's line.
- *Proposal:* If the Handler wants it strict, the POST sends each row's previewed line id and values, and the server refuses any row whose match differs. Not built.

**B3. Switching back to rules (spec 6).**
- The spec names only "a worksheet that has already been printed".
- *Built:* The row also locks at once (`"switch"`) when it already has a result, following D5 (a result is a lock event).
- Switching to "no rule" drops the row's lock ("a row switched to no rule is not locked"). When the row has a result, the dropped lock is kept in its override history entry.

**B4. Overrides on a promoted row (spec 8: "a typed value is allowed until the row is promoted").** Ruled: build it. Built in Task 4.
- The item PATCH answers 409 with detail exactly `the row is promoted; its prep is final` when it would change any of these fields while the vial's live endotoxin analysis is promoted:
  - `prep_total_dilution`, `prep_steps`, `cartridge_type_id`;
  - `prep_rules_off`, `prep_note`.
- The promoted test is `endo_prep_rules.promoted_sample_ids`, the same notion as Re-apply's refusal.
- A value sent as stored is no change. Readings and every other field keep today's behaviour.
- After a retest, the vial's live row is the new, unpromoted one, so the row is editable again.
- Part C can show the same text, or disable those inputs on a promoted row.

**B5. A row with a reading but no lock.**
- `row_rule` follows the contract: an unlocked row uses today's match. Dennis's `ruleOf` freezes such a row to the standard.
- In Mk1 every reading locks, so this happens only on data from before slice 5: UAT stacks, since v2 ships in the same release.
- Part C's `rowRule` must do the same, for promote parity.

**B6. Removed `worksheets_endo._step1_ratio`.** Nothing else used it; its logic is `endo_prep_rules._typed_values`.
- `STEP1_STANDARD_RATIO` stays. Task 1's `test_constants_match_the_contract_and_the_step1_floor_check` pins `endo_prep_rules.STEP1_RATIO` to it.
- The first draft removed it too, which would have broken that test once the part A draft arrived.
- `STEP3_RATIO` stays.

**B7. The rule's cartridge on the server.**
- `effective_values` returns the rule's cartridge without checking it against the method's allowed list. No server check reads the effective cartridge: a reading stores its cartridge on the row, and `_plan` reads the row's.
- The browser's "when it is allowed" filter stays with part C.

**B8. A result lock on a printed sheet (spec 6, Release).**
- *Problem:* Take a reading typed before the first print. The row locks with `"result"`, and the print then keeps that lock, so the paper shows its line. Read literally, clearing the reading afterwards releases the lock. The row would then follow live edits while the bench holds paper for it. D5 exists to stop exactly that.
- *Built:* The item PATCH calls `release_on_clear` only while `worksheet.printed_at` is null. `release_on_clear` itself keeps the contract and releases only a `"result"` lock.
- Pinned in `test_the_first_reading_locks_its_row_and_clearing_releases_only_a_result_lock` (row b).

### A.3 Part C (Tasks 6-9)

**Dry run.** All four tasks were applied in order in a scratch worktree of c9ccd773 (`C:/tmp/s5-draft-C`), with part A's fixture copied in.
- Every step's tests failed first, then passed.
- After each task, `tsc` passed, eslint was clean (`api.ts` keeps its 7 base errors) and prettier was clean.
- `ast-grep scan` was clean on every changed file.
- The full vitest suite has 4 failures, and the same 4 fail at c9ccd773: `App.test.tsx` 'renders main window layout', `PackagingPanel.test.tsx`, `peptide-requests-list.test.tsx` and `worksheets-inbox-lanes.test.tsx`. The two flags suites time out only when the whole suite runs, and pass on their own.
- A sheet built with no rules was diffed against c9ccd773 (HTML, preview and CSV): byte for byte identical.

**Contract deviations.**
1. **`EndoSample.rule` is optional (`rule?: EndoPrepRule | null`), not required.** `tsc -p .` checks the test files, and several existing tests build `EndoSample` literals without it: `endo-prep-2ml.test.ts`, `endo-worksheet-2ml.test.ts`, `endo-bench-sheet-2ml.test.ts`, and `STANDARD_SAMPLE`. For the same reason every new field on `EndoRow`, `EndoSheet2Row`, `EndoSheet2Doc` and `EndoBoxProps` is optional. `EndoStepPlan.ruled` is required, because no test builds that type.
2. **`rowRule` takes `item: Pick<WorksheetItemRow, 'prep_rule' | 'prep_rules_off'>` and `q: Omit<MatchQuery, 'rulesOff'>`.** Every `WorksheetItemRow` satisfies the `Pick`, and the `q` keys are the contract's. Nothing that calls it per the contract breaks.

**Rulings to confirm.**
3. **"1:300 · rule".** The 2 mL sheet has no total column (Dennis took it off paper), so a ruled 1:300 total prints through step 2's derived ratio, as `1:15 &middot; rule`. A ruled step 1 ratio prints as `1:5 &middot; rule`. This follows Dennis efbc160. The spec's "1:300" reads as the example total.
4. **"Steps that come from a rule must not be boxed as non-standard."** Built as the dispatch says.
   - A ruled step is never `own`: it is not underlined on paper, not tinted or titled "Set by hand" on screen, and not counted as typed.
   - It is also not boxed on paper: `boxOf`'s `diff` excludes a step that is ruled and not `own`. Its note still prints "1:15 · rule".
   - Dennis's own final sheet (446bb4b) does box ruled steps. There, the box warns the bench off the heading's 230 µL.
   - The Handler's alternative is to keep the box. That is a one-line revert in `boxOf`, back to `const diff = stepDiffers(st, std[si])`, plus dropping the two "not boxed" assertions in the marks test.
   - A side effect: a sheet whose only off-standard steps are ruled still prints the readnote's "A boxed step is not the standard prep" sentence, with no box on the page.
5. **Box 2's step 1 wire change, behaviour with an empty rules table.** A step 1 sample volume typed on its own now sends `[30, null]`, where it sent `[30, 10]` before.
   - The server reads both as the 1:10 standard (`_step1_ratio`), so every number is unchanged.
   - The stored value differs, though. Without the change, a ratio nobody typed is stored as typed, and it would block every future rule on that row.
   - No existing test pins the old form.
6. **The flyout summary is not given the rules.** It counts only verdicts and Accu-Mk1 values, and both need a reading. A row with a reading is locked at its first result, so it reads its lock and never the live list.
7. **No rule columns in the 2 mL CSV.** The spec asks for none; the sheet and Box 2's history carry the rule.
8. **The history view shows when and why, but not who.** `EndoBoxProps` carries no user list, and the audit row has the user. Add `users` to the props if the lab wants names.
9. **The "locked" tag appears only on a ruled row.** A row locked to the standard shows no tag, so after a print with an empty rules table the screen is unchanged. Re-apply still lists such a row when a rule now matches it.

**Notes the other parts must know.**
- **Part B: `row_facts` `is_bac` must be the form rule** that `worksheets_endo_promote` already uses: `item.prep_form == "bac"` when `prep_form` is set, else the sample-based check. The browser uses `formOf`, and the fixture cannot catch a mismatch.
  - The browser also never rules a bac row, even one locked before its form was set to bac. The Python effective values should skip the rule for bac in the same way.
- **Part B: a "no rule" row that already has a result, switched back to rules on a worksheet that has not been printed, gets no lock** under spec 6's "switch" event, which fires only after a print. Both sides would then put a row with a reading under the live rules. Locking on switch whenever the row has a result closes that gap.
- **Part B and Task 10: the browser writes the rule's cartridge onto the row the moment a reading is saved.** Box 3's `saveReading` sends the row's resolved `cartridge_type_id` with the reading, and the reader import does the same.
  - From then on the rule's cartridge reads as typed: Re-apply cannot move it, and the R no longer prints.
  - That first PATCH carries `cartridge_type_id` and `result_reading` together on a row with no result yet. It must not count as an "override" for history.
- **Part B / Task 5: the Re-apply dialog also filters candidates itself.** It drops "no rule" rows, and rows whose changed fields are all typed (Review Focus 2). When the preview applies the same rule, the button's count matches the server's.
- **Task 10 (gates).** The preview query key is `[...worksheetPageKey(id), 'prep-rules-reapply']`, so every existing `['worksheets-list']` invalidation refreshes it. Three browser checks need a stack:
  - the rules page against Task 2's routes, including the floor 400;
  - a printed ruled sheet with no page spill;
  - Re-apply against Task 5's routes.
